## Zechariah 14:7 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_nvzs)

ה֛וּא יִוָּדַ֥ע לַֽ⁠יהוָ֖ה

**"it, it will be known to Yahweh"**

The expression **will be known** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[Yahweh will know about it]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:7
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:7 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_oble)

לֹא־י֣וֹם וְ⁠לֹא־לָ֑יְלָה

**"not day and not night"**

Zechariah is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[it will not be day and it will not be night]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:7
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:7 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_fytg)

לֹא־י֣וֹם וְ⁠לֹא־לָ֑יְלָה

**"not day and not night"**

Zechariah is using the two parts of a full day, **day** and **night**, to mean the entire cycle of time. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[unlike any ordinary period of time]

See: Merism

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:7
* **Associated Articles:** Merism (ID: `33291@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:7 (#4) (id: en_tn_ZEC_fzp7)

לֹא־י֣וֹם וְ⁠לֹא־לָ֑יְלָה וְ⁠הָיָ֥ה לְ⁠עֵֽת־עֶ֖רֶב יִֽהְיֶה־אֽוֹר

**"not day and not night. And it will happen, at the time of evening there will be light"**

Zechariah states **And it will happen** here to indicate a contrast between the darkness that would normally be expected at evening and the light that will be present instead. In your translation, do not try to explain or fix this contract, instead indicate this contrast in a way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[there will be neither day nor night, but at the time of evening there will be light]

See: Connect — Contrast Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:7
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Contrast Relationship (ID: `33329@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:8 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_i0ai)

בַּ⁠קַּ֥יִץ וּ⁠בָ⁠חֹ֖רֶף

**"in the summer and in the winter"**

Zechariah is using the two main seasons of the year, **the summer** and **the winter**, to mean the whole year. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[all year long]

See: Merism

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:8
* **Associated Articles:** Merism (ID: `33291@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:8 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_mqz4)

מַֽיִם־חַיִּים֙

**"living waters"**

Zechariah is using **living waters** as a common expression of the culture to mean “fresh water that flows continually” and “water that gives life.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[continually flowing fresh streams] or \[life\-giving water]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:8
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:9 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_rdsx)

יִהְיֶ֧ה יְהוָ֛ה אֶחָ֖ד וּ⁠שְׁמ֥⁠וֹ אֶחָֽד

**"Yahweh will be one, and his name, one"**

The implication is that people everywhere will worship Yahweh alone and honor him alone as God. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[Yahweh will be the only God whom people worship, and his name, the only name that they honor]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:9
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:9 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_axqr)

וּ⁠שְׁמ֥⁠וֹ אֶחָֽד

**"and his name, one"**

Yahweh is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. He means that his name will be one, that is, unique. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[and his name will be one, meaning unique]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:9
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:9 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_kufl)

וּ⁠שְׁמ֥⁠וֹ

**"and his name"**

Here, **his name** represents Yahweh himself and his reputation. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[his reputation will be unique]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:9
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:10 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_xors)

מִ⁠גֶּ֣בַע לְ⁠רִמּ֔וֹן נֶ֖גֶב יְרֽוּשָׁלִָ֑ם

**"from Geba to Rimmon, south of Jerusalem"**

Zechariah is using the two extremes of the land of Judah, **Geba** in the north and **Rimmon** in the south, to mean all of the land between them. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[from one end of the land to the other]

See: Merism

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:10
* **Associated Articles:** Merism (ID: `33291@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin Gate (ID: `place:BenjaminGate`); Tower of Hananel (ID: `place:TowerOfHananel`); Ain (ID: `place:Ain`); Corner Gate (ID: `place:CornerGate`); Arabah (ID: `place:Arabah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Geba (ID: `place:Geba`); Winepress (ID: `realia:Winepress`)

## Zechariah 14:10 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_quob)

יִסּ֨וֹב כָּל־הָ⁠אָ֤רֶץ כָּ⁠עֲרָבָה֙

**"All the land will turn like the Arabah"**

The point of this comparison is that just as **the Arabah** is a flat plain, so the land around Jerusalem will become flat. If it would be helpful in your language, you could make this point explicitly. Alternate translation: \[All the land will become as flat as the Arabah plain]

See: Simile

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:10
* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:10 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_lodg)

וְֽ⁠רָאֲמָה֩ וְ⁠יָשְׁבָ֨ה תַחְתֶּ֜י⁠הָ

**"But it will rise up and will dwell in its place"**

The pronoun **it** refers to Jerusalem, not to the land that will become flat. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[But Jerusalem will rise up and will dwell in its place]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:10
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:10 (#4) (id: en_tn_ZEC_fxtm)

לְ⁠מִ⁠שַּׁ֣עַר בִּנְיָמִ֗ן עַד־מְק֞וֹם שַׁ֤עַר הָֽ⁠רִאשׁוֹן֙ עַד־שַׁ֣עַר הַ⁠פִּנִּ֔ים וּ⁠מִגְדַּ֣ל חֲנַנְאֵ֔ל עַ֖ד יִקְבֵ֥י הַ⁠מֶּֽלֶךְ

**"from the Gate of Benjamin to the place of the First Gate, to the Gate of the Corners, and {from} the Tower of Hananel to the winepresses of the king"**

The words **the Gate of Benjamin**, **the First Gate**, **the Gate of the Corners**, and **the Tower of Hananel** are the names of places in Jerusalem.

See: How to Translate Names

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:10
* **Associated Articles:** How to Translate Names (ID: `33391@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin Gate (ID: `place:BenjaminGate`); Tower of Hananel (ID: `place:TowerOfHananel`); Ain (ID: `place:Ain`); Corner Gate (ID: `place:CornerGate`); Arabah (ID: `place:Arabah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Geba (ID: `place:Geba`); Winepress (ID: `realia:Winepress`)

## Zechariah 14:10 (#5) (id: en_tn_ZEC_gaw7)

לְ⁠מִ⁠שַּׁ֣עַר בִּנְיָמִ֗ן עַד־מְק֞וֹם שַׁ֤עַר הָֽ⁠רִאשׁוֹן֙ עַד־שַׁ֣עַר הַ⁠פִּנִּ֔ים וּ⁠מִגְדַּ֣ל חֲנַנְאֵ֔ל עַ֖ד יִקְבֵ֥י הַ⁠מֶּֽלֶךְ

**"from the Gate of Benjamin to the place of the First Gate, to the Gate of the Corners, and {from} the Tower of Hananel to the winepresses of the king"**

Zechariah is referring to all of Jerusalem by naming two extremes, **the Gate of Benjamin** and **the winepresses of the king**. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[throughout the whole city]

See: Merism

* **Associated Articles:** Merism (ID: `33291@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:11 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_hmij)

וְ⁠יָ֣שְׁבוּ בָ֔⁠הּ

**"And they will dwell in it"**

The pronoun **they** refers to the people of Jerusalem, and the pronoun **it** refers to the city of Jerusalem. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And her people will dwell in Jerusalem]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:11 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_npfr)

וְ⁠חֵ֖רֶם לֹ֣א יִֽהְיֶה־ע֑וֹד

**"and there will not again be destruction"**

The implication is that enemies will never again devote Jerusalem to complete destruction in war. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[and no enemy will ever again devote it to destruction]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:11

וְ⁠חֵ֖רֶם לֹ֣א יִֽהְיֶה־ע֑וֹד

**"and there will not again be destruction"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **destruction**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[and no one will destroy it again]

## Zechariah 14:11 (#4) (id: en_tn_ZEC_ytky)

וְ⁠יָשְׁבָ֥ה יְרוּשָׁלִַ֖ם לָ⁠בֶֽטַח

**"and Jerusalem will dwell in security"**

**Jerusalem** is spoken of as a living thing. By referring to the city in this way, the meaning is that its name represents the people who live in Jerusalem. וְ⁠יָשְׁבָ֥ה יְרוּשָׁלִַ֖ם לָ⁠בֶֽטַח

**"and Jerusalem will dwell in security"**

## Zechariah 14:12 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_jhfb)

בְּשָׂר֗⁠וֹ וְ⁠הוּא֙ עֹמֵ֣ד עַל־רַגְלָ֔י⁠ו וְ⁠עֵינָי⁠ו֙ תִּמַּ֣קְנָה בְ⁠חֹֽרֵי⁠הֶ֔ן וּ⁠לְשׁוֹנ֖⁠וֹ תִּמַּ֥ק בְּ⁠פִי⁠הֶֽם

**"his flesh \& while he is standing on his feet, and his eyes will rot in their sockets, and his tongue will rot in their mouth"**

The pronouns **he** and **his** refer to each individual among the peoples who waged war against Jerusalem, while the pronoun **their** refers to those same peoples together as a group. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[each person’s flesh will rot away while he is standing on his feet, and his eyes will rot in their sockets, and his tongue will rot in his mouth]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:12
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:12 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_h5nz)

וְ⁠הוּא֙ עֹמֵ֣ד עַל־רַגְלָ֔י⁠ו

**"while he is standing on his feet"**

Zechariah is using **while he is standing on his feet** as a common expression of the culture to mean “while he is still alive.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[while he is yet living]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:12
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:13 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_sqsu)

מְהֽוּמַת־יְהוָ֛ה רַבָּ֖ה

**"a great tumult of Yahweh"**

Zechariah is using the possessive form **a great tumult of Yahweh** to describe a tumult that Yahweh himself will cause. If a speaker of your language would not use the possessive form for this, you could express this idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[a great tumult that Yahweh sends]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:13
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:13 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_evi7)

וְ⁠הֶחֱזִ֗יקוּ אִ֚ישׁ יַ֣ד רֵעֵ֔⁠הוּ

**"and each man will seize the hand of his neighbor"**

Here the masculine term **man** could refer to: (1\) soldiers of the enemy armies, Alternate translation: \[and each soldier will seize the hand of his neighbor] or (2\) have a generic sense that includes both men and women. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could use language in your translation that is clearly inclusive of both men and women. Alternate translation: \[and each person will seize the hand of his neighbor]

See: When Masculine Words Include Women

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:13
* **Associated Articles:** When Masculine Words Include Women (ID: `33276@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:13 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_rfxu)

וְ⁠הֶחֱזִ֗יקוּ אִ֚ישׁ יַ֣ד רֵעֵ֔⁠הוּ

**"and each man will seize the hand of his neighbor"**

Zechariah is using **each man will seize the hand of his neighbor** as a common expression of the culture to mean that people will panic and attack one another in confusion. If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[everyone will grab hold of the person next to them in panic] or \[everyone will turn against the person next to them in confusion]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:13
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:13 (#4) (id: en_tn_ZEC_l660)

וְ⁠עָלְתָ֥ה יָד֖⁠וֹ עַל־יַ֥ד רֵעֵֽ⁠הוּ

**"and his hand will go up against the hand of his neighbor"**

Zechariah is using **his hand will go up against** as a common expression of the culture to mean “he will attack.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[he will attack his neighbor]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:13
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Zechariah 14:14 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_patj)

וְ⁠גַ֨ם־יְהוּדָ֔ה תִּלָּחֵ֖ם בִּ⁠ירֽוּשָׁלִָ֑ם

**"And also Judah will fight at Jerusalem"**

The implication is that the people of Judah will take part in this battle. This could mean: (1\) that they will fight at Jerusalem alongside its people against the nations. Alternate translation: \[And also Judah will join Jerusalem in fighting against the nations] or (2\) that they will fight against Jerusalem along with the nations. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the people of Judah will also join the nations in fighting against Jerusalem]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:14
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Zechariah 14:14 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_bwo8)

וְ⁠אֻסַּף֩ חֵ֨יל כָּל־הַ⁠גּוֹיִ֜ם סָבִ֗יב

**"And the wealth of all the nations around will be gathered"**

The expression **will be gathered** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[they will collect the wealth of all the nations around]

לָ⁠רֹ֥ב מְאֹֽד

**"in very great abundance"**

## Zechariah 14:15 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_whly)

מַגֵּפַ֣ת הַ⁠סּ֗וּס הַ⁠פֶּ֨רֶד֙ הַ⁠גָּמָ֣ל וְ⁠הַ⁠חֲמ֔וֹר

**"the plague of the horse, the mule, the camel, and the donkey"**

Zechariah is using the possessive form **the plague of the horse, the mule, the camel, and the donkey** to describe the plague that will strike those animals. If a speaker of your language would not use the possessive form for this, you could express this idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[the plague that will strike the horse, the mule, the camel, and the donkey]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:15
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Mule (ID: `fauna:Mule`)

## Zechariah 14:15 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_urtn)

הַ⁠סּ֗וּס הַ⁠פֶּ֨רֶד֙ הַ⁠גָּמָ֣ל וְ⁠הַ⁠חֲמ֔וֹר

**"the horse, the mule, the camel, and the donkey"**

Zechariah is not referring to a specific **horse**, **mule**, **camel**, or **donkey**. He means horses, mules, camels, and donkeys in general. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could use an expression that would be more natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[the horses, mules, camels, and donkeys]

See: Generic Noun Phrases

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:15
* **Associated Articles:** Generic Noun Phrases (ID: `33277@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Mule (ID: `fauna:Mule`)

## Zechariah 14:16 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_zrvn)

לְ⁠הִֽשְׁתַּחֲוֺת֙ לְ⁠מֶ֨לֶךְ֙

**"to bow down to the King"**

Bowing down before someone was a symbolic action by which a person showed honor and submission to a ruler. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could explain the significance of this action in the text of your translation or in a footnote. Alternate translation: \[to bow down in honor and submission to the King]

See: Symbolic Action

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:16
* **Associated Articles:** Symbolic Action (ID: `33402@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Feast of Tabernacles (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfTabernacles`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:16 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_vppz)

אֶת־חַ֥ג הַ⁠סֻּכּֽוֹת

**"the Festival of Booths"**

The **Festival of Booths** is a kind of yearly celebration during which the Israelites lived in temporary shelters for a week to remember how their ancestors lived after they came out of Egypt. If your readers would not be familiar with what the Festival of Booths is, you could use the name of a similar thing that your readers would recognize, or you could use a more general term. Alternate translation: \[the yearly festival of living in temporary huts]

See: Translate Unknowns

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:16
* **Associated Articles:** Translate Unknowns (ID: `33408@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Feast of Tabernacles (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfTabernacles`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:17 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_d0iu)

אֲשֶׁ֨ר לֹֽא־יַעֲלֶ֜ה מֵ⁠אֵ֨ת מִשְׁפְּח֤וֹת הָ⁠אָ֨רֶץ֙ אֶל־יְר֣וּשָׁלִַ֔ם לְ⁠הִֽשְׁתַּחֲוֺ֔ת לְ⁠מֶ֖לֶךְ יְהוָ֣ה צְבָא֑וֹת וְ⁠לֹ֥א עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֖ם יִהְיֶ֥ה הַ⁠גָּֽשֶׁם

**"whoever does not go up from the clans of the earth to Jerusalem to bow down to the King, Yahweh of Armies—on them there will not be the rain"**

Zechariah is presenting a possible condition, that some of the clans of the earth will not go up to Jerusalem to worship Yahweh. Use a natural form in your language for introducing a condition that could happen. Alternate translation: \[if any of the clans of the earth do not go up to Jerusalem to bow down to the King, Yahweh of Armies, then on them there will not be the rain]

See: Connect — Hypothetical Conditions

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:17
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Hypothetical Conditions (ID: `33327@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:17 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_syc6)

וְ⁠לֹ֥א עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֖ם יִהְיֶ֥ה הַ⁠גָּֽשֶׁם

**"on them there will not be the rain"**

The implication is that without rain the crops of those people will fail, and so this will be a punishment for them. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[on them there will not be the rain, and as a punishment their crops will not grow]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:17
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Zechariah 14:18 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_zpu2)

לֹֽא־תַעֲלֶ֛ה וְ⁠לֹ֥א בָאָ֖ה

**"does not go up and does not come"**

The terms **does not go up** and **does not come** mean similar things. Zechariah is using the two terms together for emphasis. If it would be clearer for your readers, you could express the emphasis with a single phrase. Alternate translation: \[does not come at all]

See: Doublet

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:18
* **Associated Articles:** Doublet (ID: `33266@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Feast of Tabernacles (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfTabernacles`)

## Zechariah 14:18 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_hn20)

וְ⁠לֹ֣א עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֑ם

**"then not on them"**

This could mean: (1\) Yahweh will judge Egypt by plague instead of withholding rain, since rain is not how Egypt grows crops. See the UST. (2\) Yahweh will also withhold rain from southern Egypt that feeds the Nile River that the people of Egypt water their crops from rather than from direct rain. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[then the rain will not replenish the Nile either]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:18
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Plague (ID: `keyterm:Blow`); Feast of Tabernacles (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfTabernacles`)

## Zechariah 14:19 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_kp8r)

זֹ֥את תִּהְיֶ֖ה חַטַּ֣את מִצְרָ֑יִם וְ⁠חַטַּאת֙ כָּל־הַ⁠גּוֹיִ֔ם

**"This will be the sin of Egypt and the sin of all the nations"**

Here, **the sin** represents the punishment for sin. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[This will be the punishment of Egypt and the punishment of all the nations]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:19
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Punishment (ID: `keyterm:Punishment`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Feast of Tabernacles (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfTabernacles`)

## Zechariah 14:20 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_ktai)

עַל־מְצִלּ֣וֹת הַ⁠סּ֔וּס קֹ֖דֶשׁ לַֽ⁠יהוָ֑ה

**"on the bells of the horse, “Holy to Yahweh"**

The implication is that things which were very common will become holy. Someone will engrave the words “Holy to Yahweh” on the bells, just as those words were engraved on the turban of the high priest. To transfer something so holy to something so common would have been somewhat shocking to the original audience. You could include this information if that would be helpful to your readers. Alternate translation: \[on the common bells of the horse, someone will carve an inscription reading, “Holy to Yahweh]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:20
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Cooking Pot (ID: `realia:CookingPot`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Zechariah 14:20 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_ldcb)

וְ⁠הָיָ֤ה הַ⁠סִּירוֹת֙ בְּ⁠בֵ֣ית יְהוָ֔ה כַּ⁠מִּזְרָקִ֖ים לִ⁠פְנֵ֥י הַ⁠מִּזְבֵּֽחַ

**"And the pots in the house of Yahweh will be like the bowls to the face of the altar"**

The point of this comparison is that just as **the bowls** that the priests use at the altar are sacred, so **the pots in the house of Yahweh** will be sacred. If it would be helpful in your language, you could make this point explicitly. Alternate translation: \[And the pots in the house of Yahweh will be just as holy as the bowls that are before the altar]

See: Simile

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:20
* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Cooking Pot (ID: `realia:CookingPot`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Zechariah 14:20 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_n9xd)

לִ⁠פְנֵ֥י הַ⁠מִּזְבֵּֽחַ

**"to the face of the altar"**

Here, **the face of the altar** represents “the front of the altar.” If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[before the altar]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:20
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:21 (#1) (id: en_tn_ZEC_yg6t)

וְ⁠לָקְח֥וּ מֵ⁠הֶ֖ם וּ⁠בִשְּׁל֣וּ בָ⁠הֶ֑ם

**"and will take from them and will boil in them"**

The pronoun **them** refers to the pots in Jerusalem and in Judah. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[and will take a few of those pots and will boil their sacrifices in them]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:21
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Zechariah 14:21 (#2) (id: en_tn_ZEC_de9d)

כְנַעֲנִ֥י

**"a Canaanite"**

Zechariah is not referring to a specific **Canaanite**. He means Canaanites in general. If it would be helpful to your readers, you could use an expression that would be more natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[any Canaanites]

See: Generic Noun Phrases

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:21
* **Associated Articles:** Generic Noun Phrases (ID: `33277@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Cooking Pot (ID: `realia:CookingPot`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Zechariah 14:21 (#3) (id: en_tn_ZEC_ktwz)

וְ⁠לֹא־יִהְיֶ֨ה כְנַעֲנִ֥י ע֛וֹד בְּ⁠בֵית־יְהוָ֥ה צְבָא֖וֹת

**"And there will not be a Canaanite again in the house of Yahweh of Armies"**

Here, **a Canaanite** represents someone who does not belong in the house of Yahweh. You may wish to add a footnote here with the meaning you do not choose. This could mean: (1\) a trader or merchant, since the Canaanites were well known as traders, Alternate translation: \[there will not be a trader again in the house of Yahweh of Armies] or (2\) an unclean foreigner. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[there will not be a unclean foreigner again in the house of Yahweh of Armies]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Zechariah 14:21
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)
